1. # Implementation Plan: Hover Effective Resolution
2. 
3. **Branch**: `001-hover-effective-resolution` | **Date**: 2026-03-06 | **Spec**: [spec.md](spec.md)
4. **Input**: Feature specification from `/specs/001-hover-effective-resolution/spec.md`
5. 
6. ## Summary
7. 
8. Add full W×H effective and native resolution display (plus scaling
9. percentage) to both the tray icon tooltip and the top of the
10. right-click context menu. The app already calculates effective width
11. via DPI scaling; this feature extends that to include height and
12. surfaces all three data points (effective W×H, native W×H, scaling %)
13. in both UI locations.
14. 
15. ## Technical Context
16. 
17. **Language/Version**: C# / .NET 8.0 (`net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0`)

20. **Testing**: No test framework in project (manual verification)

22. **Project Type**: Desktop system-tray app
23. **Performance Goals**: Negligible overhead on existing poll cycle
24. **Constraints**: Tooltip ≤128 chars; no new P/Invoke calls needed;
25.   HKCU only; ≤30 MB memory

31. design.*

36. | II. Self-Contained | ✅ Pass | No external services, no network, no new runtime deps. |
37. | III. Pragmatic Code | ✅ Pass | Extends existing `GetWidth()` pattern to include height. No new abstractions. |
38. | IV. Startup-Ready | ✅ Pass | No impact on startup behavior or registration. |
39. | V. Store-Distributable | ✅ Pass | No new P/Invoke calls. Same `HKCU` scope. No WACK impact. |

1. # Feature Specification: Hover Effective Resolution
2. 
3. **Feature Branch**: `001-hover-effective-resolution`
4. **Created**: 2026-03-06
5. **Status**: Draft
6. **Input**: User description: "Show effective resolution (W×H) in
7. tray tooltip on hover AND at the top of the right-click context
8. menu. Effective = native resolution reduced by display scaling."
9. 
10. ## User Scenarios & Testing *(mandatory)*
11. 
12. ### User Story 1 — See Effective Resolution on Hover (Priority: P1)
13. 
14. A user docks their laptop to an external monitor and wants to
15. quickly confirm what effective resolution the tool is using to
16. select a profile. They hover over the tray icon and immediately
17. see the full effective resolution (W×H), the native resolution
18. (W×H), and the current scaling factor — without opening any menu.
19. 
20. **Why this priority**: The tooltip is the fastest way to check
21. resolution at a glance. Showing W×H (not just width) gives
22. complete context that matches how users think about resolution.
23. 
24. **Independent Test**: Hover over the tray icon on a display with
25. non-100% scaling. Verify the tooltip shows effective W×H, native
26. W×H, and scaling percentage, all matching Windows Display Settings.
27. 
28. **Acceptance Scenarios**:
29. 
30. 1. **Given** the app is running on a 3840×2160 display at 200%
31.    scaling, **When** the user hovers over the tray icon, **Then**
32.    the tooltip displays effective resolution (1920×1080), native
33.    resolution (3840×2160), and the scaling factor (200%).
34. 2. **Given** the app is running on a 1920×1080 display at 100%
35.    scaling, **When** the user hovers over the tray icon, **Then**
36.    the tooltip displays effective resolution (1920×1080) and
37.    indicates scaling is 100%.
38. 3. **Given** the user undocks from an external monitor and the
39.    laptop's built-in 2560×1600 display at 150% scaling becomes
40.    primary, **When** the user hovers over the tray icon, **Then**
41.    the tooltip updates to show effective resolution (1707×1067),
42.    native resolution (2560×1600), and scaling (150%).
43. 
44. ---
45. 
46. ### User Story 2 — Resolution Details in Context Menu (Priority: P1)
47. 
48. A user right-clicks the tray icon and sees the current resolution
49. breakdown at the top of the context menu, providing a persistent
50. reference that does not disappear like a tooltip. This information
51. appears above the existing menu items.
52. 
53. **Why this priority**: The user explicitly requested the info on
54. both hover and click. The context menu is the primary interaction
55. surface; resolution details at the top give immediate visibility
56. every time the menu opens.
57. 
58. **Independent Test**: Right-click the tray icon. Verify the top of
59. the context menu shows effective W×H, native W×H, and scaling
60. percentage matching the actual display settings.
61. 
62. **Acceptance Scenarios**:
63. 
64. 1. **Given** the app is running on a 3840×2160 display at 200%
65.    scaling, **When** the user right-clicks the tray icon, **Then**
66.    the top of the context menu shows effective resolution
67.    (1920×1080), native resolution (3840×2160), and scaling (200%).
68. 2. **Given** the display scaling changes while the app is running,
69.    **When** the user right-clicks the tray icon, **Then** the
70.    context menu reflects the updated values on next poll cycle.
71. 3. **Given** the context menu is open, **When** the user reads the
72.    resolution items, **Then** the existing menu items (Open Config,
73.    Reload Config, Run at Startup, etc.) appear below the resolution
74.    details, separated by a divider.
75. 
76. ---
77. 
78. ### Edge Cases
79. 
80. - What happens when the scaling factor is a non-standard value
81.   (e.g., 125%, 175%)? The displayed percentage MUST match the
82.   actual system DPI scaling, rounded to the nearest whole percent.
83. - What happens when the primary display cannot be detected (e.g.,
84.   headless or remote desktop with no physical monitor)? The tooltip
85.   and context menu MUST show a graceful fallback message such as
86.   "Resolution: unavailable" rather than crashing or showing stale
87.   data.
88. - What happens when effective and native resolution are identical
89.   (100% scaling)? Both the tooltip and context menu MUST still show
90.   both W×H values and indicate 100% scaling for consistency.
91. - What happens with fractional scaling values like 137.5%? The
92.   display MUST round the percentage to the nearest whole number
93.   and both width and height to the nearest whole pixel.
94. - What happens when the tooltip text exceeds the 128-character
95.   system limit? The format MUST be concise enough to fit; if the
96.   profile name is long, it may be truncated with an ellipsis to
97.   keep resolution data intact.
98. 
99. ## Requirements *(mandatory)*
100. 
101. ### Functional Requirements
102. 
103. - **FR-001**: The tray icon tooltip MUST display the current
104.   effective resolution as W×H in pixels.
105. - **FR-002**: The tray icon tooltip MUST display the native
106.   (physical) resolution of the primary display as W×H in pixels.
107. - **FR-003**: The tray icon tooltip MUST display the current display
108.   scaling factor as a percentage (e.g., "125%", "200%").
109. - **FR-004**: The context menu MUST display the effective resolution
110.   (W×H), native resolution (W×H), and scaling percentage at the
111.   top of the menu, above all existing items.
112. - **FR-005**: The context menu resolution items MUST be separated
113.   from the rest of the menu by a visual divider.
114. - **FR-006**: All displayed resolution values MUST update
115.   automatically when the primary display changes (monitor switch,
116.   dock/undock, scaling change) within the existing poll interval.
117. - **FR-007**: The tooltip MUST remain within the operating system's
118.   tooltip character limit (currently 128 characters on Windows).
119. - **FR-008**: The tooltip and context menu MUST display whole-number
120.   pixel values and whole-number percentages (rounding to nearest).
121. 
122. ### Key Entities
123. 
124. - **Display Info**: Represents the current primary display state —
125.   native resolution (W×H physical pixels), effective resolution
126.   (W×H logical pixels after scaling), and scaling factor
127.   (percentage).
128. 
129. ## Success Criteria *(mandatory)*
130. 
131. ### Measurable Outcomes
132. 
133. - **SC-001**: Users can determine the effective resolution (W×H),
134.   native resolution (W×H), and scaling factor of their primary
135.   display by hovering over the tray icon, without opening any
136.   menu.
137. - **SC-002**: Users can see the same resolution breakdown at the
138.   top of the right-click context menu every time they open it.
139. - **SC-003**: The displayed resolution values match the values shown
140.   in Windows Display Settings for the primary monitor in 100% of
141.   tested scaling configurations (100%, 125%, 150%, 175%, 200%,
142.   225%, 250%, 300%).
143. - **SC-004**: Resolution information updates within one poll cycle
144.   (default 5 seconds) after a display change event (dock, undock,
145.   scaling change).
146. - **SC-005**: The tooltip text fits within the system tooltip
147.   character limit and is fully readable without truncation.
148. 
149. ## Assumptions
150. 
151. - The tooltip format will follow a pattern like:
152.   `"Ultrawide | 1920×1080 effective (3840×2160 @ 200%)"` —
153.   exact wording may be refined during implementation as long as
154.   all four data points (effective W×H, native W×H, scaling, and
155.   active profile) are present.
156. - The context menu will present the resolution info as read-only
157.   (disabled/greyed) menu items at the top, consistent with the
158.   existing status item style.
159. - The existing poll/event mechanism for detecting display changes
160.   is sufficient; no new detection mechanism is needed.
161. - Only the primary display's resolution is shown (multi-monitor
162.   display is out of scope for this feature).
163. - Height values are derived by applying the same scaling formula
164.   used for width (physicalHeight × 96 / dpiY).
165.
```

</details>

**C:\Users\duncanbeard\Personal\taskbar-alignment-tool\specs\001-hover-effective-resolution\research.md**

C:\Users\duncanbeard\Personal\taskbar-alignment-tool\specs\001-hover-effective-resolution\research.md

<details>
<summary>53 lines</summary>

```
1. # Research: Hover Effective Resolution
2. 
3. **Date**: 2026-03-06
4. **Feature**: 001-hover-effective-resolution
5. 
6. ## Summary
7. 
8. No NEEDS CLARIFICATION items existed in the Technical Context.
9. All technology choices are determined by the existing codebase.
10. Research was limited to confirming existing patterns.
11. 
12. ## Findings
13. 
14. ### Display Resolution Calculation
15. 
16. - **Decision**: Reuse existing `GetDpiForMonitor` P/Invoke to
17.   derive both effective width and effective height.
18. - **Rationale**: The call already returns `dpiX` and `dpiY`. The
19.   existing code discards `dpiY` (out parameter `_`). Capturing it
20.   enables height scaling with zero additional P/Invoke overhead.
21. - **Alternatives considered**: `GetDeviceCaps` via GDI — rejected
22.   because `GetDpiForMonitor` is already in `NativeMethods.cs` and
23.   is per-monitor aware.
24. 
25. ### Tooltip Character Budget
26. 
27. - **Decision**: Keep tooltip ≤128 chars using format like:
28.   `"Ultrawide | 1920×1080 eff (3840×2160 @ 200%)"` (~50 chars).
29. - **Rationale**: Windows `NotifyIcon.Text` has a 128-character
30.   limit. Even with long profile names, the format fits comfortably.
31. - **Alternatives considered**: Multi-line tooltip via
32.   `NOTIFYICONDATA` v6 balloon — rejected as over-engineered per
33.   Constitution Principle III.
34. 
35. ### Context Menu Layout
36. 
37. - **Decision**: Replace the existing single "Width: Xpx" disabled
38.   menu item with two or three disabled items showing effective
39.   resolution, native resolution, and scaling %, followed by the
40.   existing "Profile:" item and a separator.
41. - **Rationale**: Disabled menu items at the top is the existing
42.   pattern. No new UI paradigm needed.
43. - **Alternatives considered**: Submenu for resolution details —
44.   rejected as unnecessary nesting for three data points.
45. 
46. ### Height Calculation
47. 
48. - **Decision**: Use `Screen.PrimaryScreen.Bounds.Height` for
49.   physical height, then scale with `physicalHeight * 96.0 / dpiY`.
50. - **Rationale**: Mirrors the existing width calculation exactly.
51.   `Bounds.Height` returns physical pixels in a DPI-aware app.
52. - **Alternatives considered**: None — this is the standard approach.
53.
```

</details>

C:\Users\duncanbeard\Personal\taskbar-alignment-tool\specs\001-hover-effective-resolution\data-model.md

<details>

```
1. # Data Model: Hover Effective Resolution
2. 
3. **Date**: 2026-03-06
4. **Feature**: 001-hover-effective-resolution
5. 
6. ## Entities
7. 
8. ### DisplayInfo
9. 
10. Represents the current state of the primary display as surfaced
11. to the user via tooltip and context menu. This is an ephemeral
12. data structure recomputed on each poll cycle — not persisted.
13. 
14. | Field | Type | Description |
15. |-------|------|-------------|
16. | EffectiveWidth | int | Logical width after DPI scaling (px) |
17. | EffectiveHeight | int | Logical height after DPI scaling (px) |
18. | NativeWidth | int | Physical display width (px) |
19. | NativeHeight | int | Physical display height (px) |
20. | ScalingPercent | int | Display scaling as whole % (e.g., 125) |
21. 
22. **Derivation rules**:
23. 
24. - `EffectiveWidth = NativeWidth × 96 / dpiX` (rounded to nearest int)
25. - `EffectiveHeight = NativeHeight × 96 / dpiY` (rounded to nearest int)
26. - `ScalingPercent = (int)Math.Round(dpiX / 96.0 × 100)`
27. 
28. **Identity**: Singleton — only one DisplayInfo exists at a time
29. (primary display only).
30. 
31. **Lifecycle**: Created fresh on each poll/event. No state
32. transitions — each instance is immutable and replaced entirely.
33. 
34. ## Relationships
35. 
36. - `DisplayInfo` is consumed by `TrayApplicationContext` to format
37.   the tooltip string and context menu status items.
38. - `DisplayInfo` is produced by `DisplayMonitor` which already
39.   owns the DPI detection and poll/event loop.
40. - The existing `EffectiveWidth` property on `DisplayMonitor` will
41.   be supplemented (not replaced) by the full `DisplayInfo`.
42.
```

</details>

1. # Quickstart: Hover Effective Resolution
2. 
3. **Date**: 2026-03-06
4. **Feature**: 001-hover-effective-resolution
5. 
6. ## Prerequisites
7. 
8. - Windows 11
9. - .NET 8 SDK installed
10. - A display with non-100% scaling (for full verification)
11. 
12. ## Build & Run
13. 
14. ```powershell
15. cd TaskbarAlignmentTool
16. dotnet build
17. dotnet run --project .
18. ```
19. 
20. The app starts in the system tray (notification area).
21. 
22. ## Verify Tooltip (US1)
23. 
24. 1. Hover over the tray icon.
25. 2. Confirm the tooltip shows:
26.    - Active profile name
27.    - Effective resolution as W×H (e.g., `1920×1080`)
28.    - Native resolution as W×H (e.g., `3840×2160`)
29.    - Scaling percentage (e.g., `200%`)
30. 3. Open **Windows Settings → System → Display** and confirm the
31.    values match.
32. 
33. ## Verify Context Menu (US2)
34. 
35. 1. Right-click the tray icon.
36. 2. Confirm the top of the menu shows resolution details:
37.    - Effective resolution (W×H)
38.    - Native resolution (W×H) and scaling %
39. 3. Confirm these items appear above "Open Config" and are
40.    separated by a divider.
41. 4. Confirm the items are read-only (greyed/disabled).
42. 
43. ## Verify Dynamic Update
44. 
45. 1. Change display scaling in Windows Settings (or dock/undock).
46. 2. Wait up to one poll cycle (default 5 seconds).
47. 3. Hover and right-click again — values should reflect the change.
48. 
49. ## Edge Case Checks
50. 
51. - Set scaling to 100%: effective and native should be identical.
52. - Set scaling to 125% or 175%: verify fractional results round
53.   to whole pixels and whole percent.
54. - Disconnect all external monitors: verify tooltip shows a
55.   graceful fallback if no primary display is detected.
56.
````

</details>

162. ## Dependencies & Execution Order
163. 
164. ### Phase Dependencies
165. 
166. - **Setup (Phase 1)**: No dependencies - can start immediately
167. - **Foundational (Phase 2)**: Depends on Setup completion - BLOCKS all user stories
168. - **User Stories (Phase 3+)**: All depend on Foundational phase completion
169.   - User stories can then proceed in parallel (if staffed)
170.   - Or sequentially in priority order (P1 → P2 → P3)
171. - **Polish (Final Phase)**: Depends on all desired user stories being complete
172. 
173. ### User Story Dependencies
174. 
175. - **User Story 1 (P1)**: Can start after Foundational (Phase 2) - No dependencies on other stories
176. - **User Story 2 (P2)**: Can start after Foundational (Phase 2) - May integrate with US1 but should be independently testable
177. - **User Story 3 (P3)**: Can start after Foundational (Phase 2) - May integrate with US1/US2 but should be independently testable
178. 
179. ### Within Each User Story
180. 
181. - Tests (if included) MUST be written and FAIL before implementation
182. - Models before services
183. - Services before endpoints
184. - Core implementation before integration
185. - Story complete before moving to next priority
186. 
187. ### Parallel Opportunities
188. 
189. - All Setup tasks marked [P] can run in parallel
190. - All Foundational tasks marked [P] can run in parallel (within Phase 2)
191. - Once Foundational phase completes, all user stories can start in parallel (if team capacity allows)
192. - All tests for a user story marked [P] can run in parallel
193. - Models within a story marked [P] can run in parallel
194. - Different user stories can be worked on in parallel by different team members
195. 
196. ---
197. 
198. ## Parallel Example: User Story 1
199. 
200. ```bash
201. # Launch all tests for User Story 1 together (if tests requested):
202. Task: "Contract test for [endpoint] in tests/contract/test_[name].py"
203. Task: "Integration test for [user journey] in tests/integration/test_[name].py"
204. 
205. # Launch all models for User Story 1 together:
206. Task: "Create [Entity1] model in src/models/[entity1].py"
207. Task: "Create [Entity2] model in src/models/[entity2].py"
208. ```
209. 
210. ---
211. 
212. ## Implementation Strategy
213. 
214. ### MVP First (User Story 1 Only)
215. 
216. 1. Complete Phase 1: Setup
217. 2. Complete Phase 2: Foundational (CRITICAL - blocks all stories)
218. 3. Complete Phase 3: User Story 1
219. 4. **STOP and VALIDATE**: Test User Story 1 independently
220. 5. Deploy/demo if ready
221. 
222. ### Incremental Delivery
223. 
224. 1. Complete Setup + Foundational → Foundation ready
225. 2. Add User Story 1 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo (MVP!)
226. 3. Add User Story 2 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo
227. 4. Add User Story 3 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo
228. 5. Each story adds value without breaking previous stories
229. 
230. ### Parallel Team Strategy
231. 
232. With multiple developers:
233. 
234. 1. Team completes Setup + Foundational together
235. 2. Once Foundational is done:
236.    - Developer A: User Story 1
237.    - Developer B: User Story 2
238.    - Developer C: User Story 3
239. 3. Stories complete and integrate independently
240. 
241. ---
242. 
243. ## Notes
244. 
245. - [P] tasks = different files, no dependencies
246. - [Story] label maps task to specific user story for traceability
247. - Each user story should be independently completable and testable
248. - Verify tests fail before implementing
249. - Commit after each task or logical group
250. - Stop at any checkpoint to validate story independently
251. - Avoid: vague tasks, same file conflicts, cross-story dependencies that break independence
252.
````

```
1. using Microsoft.Win32;
2. 
3. namespace TaskbarAlignmentTool;
4. 
5. /// <summary>
6. /// Monitors the primary display's effective resolution using Windows messages
7. /// (WM_DISPLAYCHANGE, WM_DPICHANGED) and a low-frequency fallback timer.
8. /// </summary>
9. internal sealed class DisplayMonitor : IDisposable
10. {
11.     private readonly System.Windows.Forms.Timer _fallbackTimer;
12.     private readonly DisplayChangeWindow _messageWindow;
13.     private int _lastWidth;
14.     private ResolutionMode _resolutionMode;
15. 
16.     public int EffectiveWidth => GetWidth(_resolutionMode);
17. 
18.     public event EventHandler<int>? ResolutionChanged;
19. 
20.     public DisplayMonitor(int fallbackIntervalMs = 60000, ResolutionMode resolutionMode = ResolutionMode.Effective)
21.     {
22.         _resolutionMode = resolutionMode;
23.         _lastWidth = EffectiveWidth;
24. 
25.         // Hidden window to receive WM_DISPLAYCHANGE and WM_DPICHANGED
26.         _messageWindow = new DisplayChangeWindow(OnDisplayMessage);
27. 
28.         // Also subscribe to the .NET event for display settings changes
29.         SystemEvents.DisplaySettingsChanged += OnDisplaySettingsChanged;
30. 
31.         // Low-frequency fallback timer (default 60s) as a safety net
32.         _fallbackTimer = new System.Windows.Forms.Timer
33.         {
34.             Interval = Math.Max(fallbackIntervalMs, 5000)
35.         };
36.         _fallbackTimer.Tick += OnFallbackTick;
37.         _fallbackTimer.Start();
38.     }
39. 
40.     /// <summary>Updates the fallback timer interval and resolution mode at runtime.</summary>
41.     public void UpdateInterval(int intervalMs, ResolutionMode? resolutionMode = null)
42.     {
43.         _fallbackTimer.Interval = Math.Max(intervalMs, 5000);
44.         if (resolutionMode.HasValue)
45.             _resolutionMode = resolutionMode.Value;
46.     }
47. 
48.     private void OnDisplayMessage()
49.     {
50.         CheckAndNotify();
51.     }
52. 
53.     private void OnDisplaySettingsChanged(object? sender, EventArgs e)
54.     {
55.         CheckAndNotify();
56.     }
57. 
58.     private void OnFallbackTick(object? sender, EventArgs e)
59.     {
60.         CheckAndNotify();
61.     }
62. 
63.     private void CheckAndNotify()
64.     {
65.         var current = EffectiveWidth;
66.         if (current != _lastWidth)
67.         {
68.             _lastWidth = current;
69.             ResolutionChanged?.Invoke(this, current);
70.         }
71.     }
72. 
73.     /// <summary>Forces a re-check and fires the event if the width changed (or always if force is true).</summary>
74.     public void Refresh(bool force = false)
75.     {
76.         var current = EffectiveWidth;
77.         if (force || current != _lastWidth)
78.         {
79.             _lastWidth = current;
80.             ResolutionChanged?.Invoke(this, current);
81.         }
82.     }
83. 
84.     private static int GetWidth(ResolutionMode mode)
85.     {
86.         var screen = Screen.PrimaryScreen;
87.         if (screen == null) return 0;
88. 
89.         int physicalWidth = screen.Bounds.Width;
90. 
91.         if (mode == ResolutionMode.Physical)
92.             return physicalWidth;
93. 
94.         try
95.         {
96.             var hMonitor = NativeMethods.MonitorFromPoint(0, NativeMethods.MONITOR_DEFAULTTOPRIMARY);
97.             if (hMonitor != nint.Zero &&
98.                 NativeMethods.GetDpiForMonitor(hMonitor, NativeMethods.MDT_EFFECTIVE_DPI, out uint dpiX, out _) == 0 &&
99.                 dpiX > 0)
100.             {
101.                 return (int)(physicalWidth * 96.0 / dpiX);
102.             }
103.         }
104.         catch
105.         {
106.             // Fall through to physical width
107.         }
108. 
109.         return physicalWidth;
110.     }
111. 
112.     public void Dispose()
113.     {
114.         SystemEvents.DisplaySettingsChanged -= OnDisplaySettingsChanged;
115.         _fallbackTimer.Stop();
116.         _fallbackTimer.Dispose();
117.         _messageWindow.DestroyHandle();
118.     }
119. 
120.     /// <summary>
121.     /// Hidden NativeWindow that receives WM_DISPLAYCHANGE and WM_DPICHANGED messages.
122.     /// </summary>
123.     private sealed class DisplayChangeWindow : NativeWindow
124.     {
125.         private readonly Action _onChange;
126. 
127.         public DisplayChangeWindow(Action onChange)
128.         {
129.             _onChange = onChange;
130.             var cp = new CreateParams
131.             {
132.                 Caption = "TaskbarAlignmentTool_DisplayMonitor",
133.                 // Message-only window (HWND_MESSAGE parent)
134.                 Parent = new nint(-3)
135.             };
136.             CreateHandle(cp);
137.         }
138. 
139.         protected override void WndProc(ref Message m)
140.         {
141.             if (m.Msg is NativeMethods.WM_DISPLAYCHANGE or NativeMethods.WM_DPICHANGED)
142.             {
143.                 _onChange();
144.             }
145.             base.WndProc(ref m);
146.         }
147.     }
148. }
149.

1. using System.Diagnostics;
2. using Microsoft.Win32;
3. 
4. namespace TaskbarAlignmentTool;
5. 
6. /// <summary>
7. /// Application context that manages the system-tray icon, context menu,
8. /// and wires the display monitor to the taskbar aligner using resolution profiles.
9. /// </summary>
10. internal sealed class TrayApplicationContext : ApplicationContext
11. {
12.     private const string StartupRegistryPath = @"Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run";
13.     private const string StartupValueName = "TaskbarAlignmentTool";
14. 
15.     private AppConfig _config;
16.     private readonly DisplayMonitor _monitor;
17.     private readonly NotifyIcon _notifyIcon;
18.     private readonly ToolStripMenuItem _statusItem;
19.     private readonly ToolStripMenuItem _profileItem;
20.     private readonly ToolStripMenuItem _startupItem;
21. 
22.     private static readonly Lazy<bool> _isMsixPackaged = new(DetectMsixPackaged);
23. 
24.     private int _profileSwitchCount;
25. 
26.     public TrayApplicationContext(AppConfig config)
27.     {
28.         _config = config;
29.         _monitor = new DisplayMonitor(config.RefreshIntervalMs, config.ResolutionMode);
30. 
31.         _statusItem = new ToolStripMenuItem("Initializing...") { Enabled = false };
32.         _profileItem = new ToolStripMenuItem("Profile: —") { Enabled = false };
33.         _startupItem = new ToolStripMenuItem("Run at Startup", null, OnToggleStartup)
34.         {
35.             Checked = IsStartupEnabled()
36.         };
37. 
38.         var menu = new ContextMenuStrip();
39.         menu.Items.Add(_statusItem);
40.         menu.Items.Add(_profileItem);
41.         menu.Items.Add(new ToolStripSeparator());
42.         menu.Items.Add("Open Config", null, OnOpenConfig);
43.         menu.Items.Add("Reload Config", null, OnReloadConfig);
44.         menu.Items.Add(new ToolStripSeparator());
45.         menu.Items.Add(_startupItem);
46.         menu.Items.Add("Refresh Now", null, OnRefresh);
47.         menu.Items.Add(new ToolStripSeparator());
48.         menu.Items.Add(CreateDiagnosticsMenu());
49.         menu.Items.Add(new ToolStripSeparator());
50.         menu.Items.Add("Exit", null, OnExit);
51. 
52.         _notifyIcon = new NotifyIcon
53.         {
54.             Icon = RenderTrayIcon(0),
55.             ContextMenuStrip = menu,
56.             Text = "Taskbar Alignment Tool",
57.             Visible = true
58.         };
59. 
60.         _monitor.ResolutionChanged += OnResolutionChanged;
61. 
62.         // Apply profile immediately on startup
63.         ApplyForWidth(_monitor.EffectiveWidth);
64.     }
65. 
66.     private void OnResolutionChanged(object? sender, int effectiveWidth)
67.     {
68.         ApplyForWidth(effectiveWidth);
69.     }
70. 
71.     private void ApplyForWidth(int effectiveWidth)
72.     {
73.         var profile = _config.ResolveProfile(effectiveWidth);
74.         if (profile != null && TaskbarAligner.ApplyProfile(profile))
75.         {
76.             _profileSwitchCount++;
77.             if (_config.ShowNotifications)
78.             {
79.                 _notifyIcon.ShowBalloonTip(
80.                     _config.NotificationDurationMs,
81.                     "Taskbar Alignment Tool",
82.                     $"Switched to \"{profile.Name}\" ({effectiveWidth}px)",
83.                     ToolTipIcon.Info);
84.             }
85.         }
86.         UpdateStatus(effectiveWidth, profile);
87.     }
88. 
89.     private void UpdateStatus(int effectiveWidth, ProfileConfig? profile)
90.     {
91.         var profileName = profile?.Name ?? "None";
92.         _statusItem.Text = $"Width: {effectiveWidth}px";
93.         _profileItem.Text = $"Profile: {profileName}";
94.         _notifyIcon.Text = $"Taskbar: {profileName} ({effectiveWidth}px)";
95. 
96.         // Update tray icon with current width
97.         var oldIcon = _notifyIcon.Icon;
98.         _notifyIcon.Icon = RenderTrayIcon(effectiveWidth);
99.         oldIcon?.Dispose();
100.     }
101. 
102.     private void OnRefresh(object? sender, EventArgs e)
103.     {
104.         _monitor.Refresh(force: true);
105.     }
106. 
107.     private void OnOpenConfig(object? sender, EventArgs e)
108.     {
109.         var path = AppConfig.GetConfigPath();
110.         if (!File.Exists(path))
111.             _config.Save();
112.         Process.Start(new ProcessStartInfo(path) { UseShellExecute = true });
113.     }
114. 
115.     private void OnReloadConfig(object? sender, EventArgs e)
116.     {
117.         _config = AppConfig.Load();
118.         _monitor.UpdateInterval(_config.RefreshIntervalMs, _config.ResolutionMode);
119.         _monitor.Refresh(force: true);
120.     }
121. 
122.     private void OnToggleStartup(object? sender, EventArgs e)
123.     {
124.         if (IsStartupEnabled())
125.             DisableStartup();
126.         else
127.             EnableStartup();
128. 
129.         _startupItem.Checked = IsStartupEnabled();
130.     }
131. 
132.     private static bool IsMsixPackaged() => _isMsixPackaged.Value;
133. 
134.     private static bool DetectMsixPackaged()
135.     {
136.         try
137.         {
138.             // Windows.ApplicationModel.Package.Current throws if not packaged
139.             _ = Windows.ApplicationModel.Package.Current.Id;
140.             return true;
141.         }
142.         catch
143.         {
144.             return false;
145.         }
146.     }
147. 
148.     private static bool IsStartupEnabled()
149.     {
150.         if (IsMsixPackaged())
151.         {
152.             try
153.             {
154.                 var task = Windows.ApplicationModel.StartupTask
155.                     .GetAsync("TaskbarAlignmentToolStartup").GetAwaiter().GetResult();
156.                 return task.State == Windows.ApplicationModel.StartupTaskState.Enabled;
157.             }
158.             catch { return false; }
159.         }
160. 
161.         using var key = Registry.CurrentUser.OpenSubKey(StartupRegistryPath, false);
162.         return key?.GetValue(StartupValueName) != null;
163.     }
164. 
165.     private static void EnableStartup()
166.     {
167.         if (IsMsixPackaged())
168.         {
169.             try
170.             {
171.                 var task = Windows.ApplicationModel.StartupTask
172.                     .GetAsync("TaskbarAlignmentToolStartup").GetAwaiter().GetResult();
173.                 task.RequestEnableAsync().GetAwaiter().GetResult();
174.             }
175.             catch { /* Startup task not available */ }
176.             return;
177.         }
178. 
179.         using var key = Registry.CurrentUser.OpenSubKey(StartupRegistryPath, true);
180.         var exePath = Application.ExecutablePath;
181.         key?.SetValue(StartupValueName, $"\"{exePath}\"");
182.     }
183. 
184.     private static void DisableStartup()
185.     {
186.         if (IsMsixPackaged())
187.         {
188.             try
189.             {
190.                 var task = Windows.ApplicationModel.StartupTask
191.                     .GetAsync("TaskbarAlignmentToolStartup").GetAwaiter().GetResult();
192.                 task.Disable();
193.             }
194.             catch { /* Startup task not available */ }
195.             return;
196.         }
197. 
198.         using var key = Registry.CurrentUser.OpenSubKey(StartupRegistryPath, true);
199.         key?.DeleteValue(StartupValueName, false);
200.     }
201. 
202.     private ToolStripMenuItem CreateDiagnosticsMenu()
203.     {
204.         var diag = new ToolStripMenuItem("Diagnostics");
205.         diag.DropDownOpening += (_, _) =>
206.         {
207.             diag.DropDownItems.Clear();
208.             var proc = System.Diagnostics.Process.GetCurrentProcess();
209.             var memMb = proc.WorkingSet64 / (1024.0 * 1024.0);
210.             var cpuTime = proc.TotalProcessorTime;
211.             diag.DropDownItems.Add(new ToolStripMenuItem($"Memory: {memMb:F1} MB") { Enabled = false });
212.             diag.DropDownItems.Add(new ToolStripMenuItem($"CPU time: {cpuTime.TotalSeconds:F2}s") { Enabled = false });
213.             diag.DropDownItems.Add(new ToolStripMenuItem($"Profile switches: {_profileSwitchCount}") { Enabled = false });
214.         };
215.         return diag;
216.     }
217. 
218.     private void OnExit(object? sender, EventArgs e)
219.     {
220.         _notifyIcon.Visible = false;
221.         _monitor.Dispose();
222.         _notifyIcon.Dispose();
223.         Application.Exit();
224.     }
225. 
226.     /// <summary>
227.     /// Renders a DPI-aware tray icon showing the current display width.
228.     /// Auto-detects Windows light/dark theme for contrast.
229.     /// </summary>
230.     private static Icon RenderTrayIcon(int width)
231.     {
232.         int dpi = GetSystemDpi();
233.         int size = (int)(16 * dpi / 96.0);
234.         float scale = size / 16f;
235.         bool isDarkTheme = IsSystemDarkTheme();
236. 
237.         using var bmp = new Bitmap(size, size);
238.         using var g = Graphics.FromImage(bmp);
239.         g.SmoothingMode = System.Drawing.Drawing2D.SmoothingMode.HighQuality;
240.         g.TextRenderingHint = System.Drawing.Text.TextRenderingHint.ClearTypeGridFit;
241.         g.PixelOffsetMode = System.Drawing.Drawing2D.PixelOffsetMode.HighQuality;
242.         g.Clear(Color.Transparent);
243. 
244.         // Background color adapts to theme
245.         var bgColor = isDarkTheme
246.             ? Color.FromArgb(255, 0, 150, 180)   // Teal on dark taskbar
247.             : Color.FromArgb(255, 0, 120, 150);  // Darker teal on light taskbar
248.         var textBrush = isDarkTheme ? Brushes.White : Brushes.White;
249. 
250.         using var bgBrush = new SolidBrush(bgColor);
251.         g.FillRectangle(bgBrush, 0, 0, size, size);
252. 
253.         // Display width as the full icon content
254.         var label = width.ToString();
255.         float fontSize = label.Length <= 3 ? 7f : label.Length == 4 ? 5.5f : 4.5f;
256.         using var font = new Font("Segoe UI", fontSize * scale, FontStyle.Bold, GraphicsUnit.Pixel);
257.         var sf = new StringFormat
258.         {
259.             Alignment = StringAlignment.Center,
260.             LineAlignment = StringAlignment.Center
261.         };
262.         var textRect = new RectangleF(0, 0, size, size);
263.         g.DrawString(label, font, textBrush, textRect, sf);
264. 
265.         return Icon.FromHandle(bmp.GetHicon());
266.     }
267. 
268.     /// <summary>
269.     /// Checks if Windows is using dark mode for apps.
270.     /// Registry: HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Themes\Personalize\SystemUsesLightTheme
271.     /// 0 = dark, 1 = light
272.     /// </summary>
273.     private static bool IsSystemDarkTheme()
274.     {
275.         try
276.         {
277.             using var key = Microsoft.Win32.Registry.CurrentUser.OpenSubKey(
278.                 @"Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Themes\Personalize", false);
279.             var value = key?.GetValue("SystemUsesLightTheme");
280.             if (value is int intVal)
281.                 return intVal == 0;
282.         }
283.         catch { }
284.         return true; // Default to dark
285.     }
286. 
287.     private static int GetSystemDpi()
288.     {
289.         try
290.         {
291.             var hMonitor = NativeMethods.MonitorFromPoint(0, NativeMethods.MONITOR_DEFAULTTOPRIMARY);
292.             if (hMonitor != nint.Zero &&
293.                 NativeMethods.GetDpiForMonitor(hMonitor, NativeMethods.MDT_EFFECTIVE_DPI, out uint dpiX, out _) == 0 &&
294.                 dpiX > 0)
295.             {
296.                 return (int)dpiX;
297.             }
298.         }
299.         catch { }
300.         return 96;
301.     }
302. }
303.

1. using System.Text.Json;
2. using System.Text.Json.Serialization;
3. 
4. namespace TaskbarAlignmentTool;
5. 
6. /// <summary>Taskbar alignment options.</summary>
7. [JsonConverter(typeof(JsonStringEnumConverter))]
8. public enum AlignmentOption { Left = 0, Center = 1 }
9. 
10. /// <summary>Combine taskbar buttons options.</summary>
11. [JsonConverter(typeof(JsonStringEnumConverter))]
12. public enum CombineButtonsOption { Always = 0, WhenFull = 1, Never = 2 }
13. 
14. /// <summary>Taskbar size options.</summary>
15. [JsonConverter(typeof(JsonStringEnumConverter))]
16. public enum TaskbarSizeOption { Small = 0, Default = 1, Large = 2 }
17. 
18. /// <summary>How to measure the display width.</summary>
19. [JsonConverter(typeof(JsonStringEnumConverter))]
20. public enum ResolutionMode { Effective, Physical }
21. 
22. /// <summary>A resolution profile that activates at a minimum effective width.</summary>
23. public sealed class ProfileConfig
24. {
25.     public string Name { get; set; } = "Default";
26.     public int MinWidth { get; set; }
27.     public AlignmentOption Alignment { get; set; } = AlignmentOption.Center;
28.     public CombineButtonsOption CombineButtons { get; set; } = CombineButtonsOption.Always;
29.     public TaskbarSizeOption TaskbarSize { get; set; } = TaskbarSizeOption.Default;
30. }
31. 
32. /// <summary>Root configuration model.</summary>
33. public sealed class AppConfig
34. {
35.     private const string SchemaUrl = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/duncanbeard/taskbar-alignment-tool/main/schema/config.schema.json";
36. 
37.     [JsonPropertyName("$schema")]
38.     public string Schema { get; set; } = SchemaUrl;
39. 
40.     public int RefreshIntervalMs { get; set; } = 60000;
41.     public bool ShowNotifications { get; set; } = true;
42.     public int NotificationDurationMs { get; set; } = 1000;
43.     public ResolutionMode ResolutionMode { get; set; } = ResolutionMode.Effective;
44.     public List<ProfileConfig> Profiles { get; set; } = [];
45. 
46.     private static readonly JsonSerializerOptions JsonOptions = new()
47.     {
48.         WriteIndented = true,
49.         PropertyNamingPolicy = JsonNamingPolicy.CamelCase,
50.         DefaultIgnoreCondition = JsonIgnoreCondition.Never
51.     };
52. 
53.     /// <summary>Returns the default config with 3 preset profiles.</summary>
54.     public static AppConfig CreateDefault() => new()
55.     {
56.         RefreshIntervalMs = 60000,
57.         ShowNotifications = true,
58.         NotificationDurationMs = 1000,
59.         ResolutionMode = ResolutionMode.Effective,
60.         Profiles =
61.         [
62.             new ProfileConfig
63.             {
64.                 Name = "Compact",
65.                 MinWidth = 0,
66.                 Alignment = AlignmentOption.Left,
67.                 CombineButtons = CombineButtonsOption.Never,
68.                 TaskbarSize = TaskbarSizeOption.Small
69.             },
70.             new ProfileConfig
71.             {
72.                 Name = "Standard",
73.                 MinWidth = 1920,
74.                 Alignment = AlignmentOption.Left,
75.                 CombineButtons = CombineButtonsOption.WhenFull,
76.                 TaskbarSize = TaskbarSizeOption.Default
77.             },
78.             new ProfileConfig
79.             {
80.                 Name = "Ultrawide",
81.                 MinWidth = 2560,
82.                 Alignment = AlignmentOption.Center,
83.                 CombineButtons = CombineButtonsOption.Always,
84.                 TaskbarSize = TaskbarSizeOption.Default
85.             }
86.         ]
87.     };
88. 
89.     /// <summary>Gets the config file path in LocalAppData (MSIX-compatible).</summary>
90.     public static string GetConfigPath()
91.     {
92.         var dir = Path.Combine(
93.             Environment.GetFolderPath(Environment.SpecialFolder.LocalApplicationData),
94.             "TaskbarAlignmentTool");
95.         Directory.CreateDirectory(dir);
96.         return Path.Combine(dir, "config.json");
97.     }
98. 
99.     /// <summary>Loads config from disk, creating a default file if it doesn't exist.
100.     /// Migrates from legacy exe-adjacent location if found.</summary>
101.     public static AppConfig Load()
102.     {
103.         var path = GetConfigPath();
104. 
105.         // Migrate from legacy exe-adjacent config if it exists
106.         MigrateLegacyConfig(path);
107. 
108.         if (!File.Exists(path))
109.         {
110.             var defaults = CreateDefault();
111.             defaults.Save();
112.             return defaults;
113.         }
114. 
115.         try
116.         {
117.             var json = File.ReadAllText(path);
118.             return JsonSerializer.Deserialize<AppConfig>(json, JsonOptions) ?? CreateDefault();
119.         }
120.         catch
121.         {
122.             return CreateDefault();
123.         }
124.     }
125. 
126.     /// <summary>Saves this config to disk.</summary>
127.     public void Save()
128.     {
129.         var path = GetConfigPath();
130.         var json = JsonSerializer.Serialize(this, JsonOptions);
131.         File.WriteAllText(path, json);
132.     }
133. 
134.     /// <summary>
135.     /// Returns the best matching profile for the given effective width.
136.     /// Profiles are matched by the highest minWidth that is &lt;= effectiveWidth.
137.     /// </summary>
138.     public ProfileConfig? ResolveProfile(int effectiveWidth)
139.     {
140.         return Profiles
141.             .Where(p => effectiveWidth >= p.MinWidth)
142.             .OrderByDescending(p => p.MinWidth)
143.             .FirstOrDefault();
144.     }
145. 
146.     private static void MigrateLegacyConfig(string newPath)
147.     {
148.         if (File.Exists(newPath))
149.             return;
150. 
151.         var legacyDir = Path.GetDirectoryName(Application.ExecutablePath) ?? ".";
152.         var legacyPath = Path.Combine(legacyDir, "config.json");
153.         if (File.Exists(legacyPath))
154.         {
155.             try
156.             {
157.                 File.Copy(legacyPath, newPath, overwrite: false);
158.                 File.Delete(legacyPath);
159.             }
160.             catch
161.             {
162.                 // Best-effort migration; ignore failures
163.             }
164.         }
165.     }
166. }
167.

5. 
6. ## Summary
7. 
8. Add full W×H effective and native resolution display (plus scaling
9. percentage) to both the tray icon tooltip and the top of the
10. right-click context menu. The app already calculates effective width
11. via DPI scaling; this feature extends that to include height and
12. surfaces all three data points (effective W×H, native W×H, scaling %)
13. in both UI locations.

1. # Feature Specification: Hover Effective Resolution
2. 
3. **Feature Branch**: `001-hover-effective-resolution`
4. **Created**: 2026-03-06
5. **Status**: Draft
6. **Input**: User description: "Show effective resolution (W×H) in
7. tray tooltip on hover AND at the top of the right-click context
8. menu. Effective = native resolution reduced by display scaling."
9. 
10. ## User Scenarios & Testing *(mandatory)*
11. 
12. ### User Story 1 — See Effective Resolution on Hover (Priority: P1)
13. 
14. A user docks their laptop to an external monitor and wants to
15. quickly confirm what effective resolution the tool is using to
16. select a profile. They hover over the tray icon and immediately
17. see the full effective resolution (W×H), the native resolution
18. (W×H), and the current scaling factor — without opening any menu.
19. 
20. **Why this priority**: The tooltip is the fastest way to check
21. resolution at a glance. Showing W×H (not just width) gives
22. complete context that matches how users think about resolution.
23. 
24. **Independent Test**: Hover over the tray icon on a display with
25. non-100% scaling. Verify the tooltip shows effective W×H, native
26. W×H, and scaling percentage, all matching Windows Display Settings.
27. 
28. **Acceptance Scenarios**:
29. 
30. 1. **Given** the app is running on a 3840×2160 display at 200%
31.    scaling, **When** the user hovers over the tray icon, **Then**
32.    the tooltip displays effective resolution (1920×1080), native
33.    resolution (3840×2160), and the scaling factor (200%).
34. 2. **Given** the app is running on a 1920×1080 display at 100%
35.    scaling, **When** the user hovers over the tray icon, **Then**
36.    the tooltip displays effective resolution (1920×1080) and
37.    indicates scaling is 100%.
38. 3. **Given** the user undocks from an external monitor and the
39.    laptop's built-in 2560×1600 display at 150% scaling becomes
40.    primary, **When** the user hovers over the tray icon, **Then**
41.    the tooltip updates to show effective resolution (1707×1067),
42.    native resolution (2560×1600), and scaling (150%).
43. 
44. ---
45. 
46. ### User Story 2 — Resolution Details in Context Menu (Priority: P1)
47. 
48. A user right-clicks the tray icon and sees the current resolution
49. breakdown at the top of the context menu, providing a persistent
50. reference that does not disappear like a tooltip. This information
51. appears above the existing menu items.
52. 
53. **Why this priority**: The user explicitly requested the info on
54. both hover and click. The context menu is the primary interaction
55. surface; resolution details at the top give immediate visibility
56. every time the menu opens.
57. 
58. **Independent Test**: Right-click the tray icon. Verify the top of
59. the context menu shows effective W×H, native W×H, and scaling
60. percentage matching the actual display settings.
61. 
62. **Acceptance Scenarios**:
63. 
64. 1. **Given** the app is running on a 3840×2160 display at 200%
65.    scaling, **When** the user right-clicks the tray icon, **Then**
66.    the top of the context menu shows effective resolution
67.    (1920×1080), native resolution (3840×2160), and scaling (200%).
68. 2. **Given** the display scaling changes while the app is running,
69.    **When** the user right-clicks the tray icon, **Then** the
70.    context menu reflects the updated values on next poll cycle.
71. 3. **Given** the context menu is open, **When** the user reads the
72.    resolution items, **Then** the existing menu items (Open Config,
73.    Reload Config, Run at Startup, etc.) appear below the resolution
74.    details, separated by a divider.
75. 
76. ---
77. 
78. ### Edge Cases
79. 
80. - What happens when the scaling factor is a non-standard value
81.   (e.g., 125%, 175%)? The displayed percentage MUST match the
82.   actual system DPI scaling, rounded to the nearest whole percent.
83. - What happens when the primary display cannot be detected (e.g.,
84.   headless or remote desktop with no physical monitor)? The tooltip
85.   and context menu MUST show a graceful fallback message such as
86.   "Resolution: unavailable" rather than crashing or showing stale
87.   data.
88. - What happens when effective and native resolution are identical
89.   (100% scaling)? Both the tooltip and context menu MUST still show
90.   both W×H values and indicate 100% scaling for consistency.
91. - What happens with fractional scaling values like 137.5%? The
92.   display MUST round the percentage to the nearest whole number
93.   and both width and height to the nearest whole pixel.
94. - What happens when the tooltip text exceeds the 128-character
95.   system limit? The format MUST be concise enough to fit; if the
96.   profile name is long, it may be truncated with an ellipsis to
97.   keep resolution data intact.
98. 
99. ## Requirements *(mandatory)*
100. 
101. ### Functional Requirements
102. 
103. - **FR-001**: The tray icon tooltip MUST display the current
104.   effective resolution as W×H in pixels.
105. - **FR-002**: The tray icon tooltip MUST display the native
106.   (physical) resolution of the primary display as W×H in pixels.
107. - **FR-003**: The tray icon tooltip MUST display the current display
108.   scaling factor as a percentage (e.g., "125%", "200%").
109. - **FR-004**: The context menu MUST display the effective resolution
110.   (W×H), native resolution (W×H), and scaling percentage at the
111.   top of the menu, above all existing items.
112. - **FR-005**: The context menu resolution items MUST be separated
113.   from the rest of the menu by a visual divider.
114. - **FR-006**: All displayed resolution values MUST update
115.   automatically when the primary display changes (monitor switch,
116.   dock/undock, scaling change) within the existing poll interval.
117. - **FR-007**: The tooltip MUST remain within the operating system's
118.   tooltip character limit (currently 128 characters on Windows).
119. - **FR-008**: The tooltip and context menu MUST display whole-number
120.   pixel values and whole-number percentages (rounding to nearest).
121. 
122. ### Key Entities
123. 
124. - **Display Info**: Represents the current primary display state —
125.   native resolution (W×H physical pixels), effective resolution
126.   (W×H logical pixels after scaling), and scaling factor
127.   (percentage).
128. 
129. ## Success Criteria *(mandatory)*
130. 
131. ### Measurable Outcomes
132. 
133. - **SC-001**: Users can determine the effective resolution (W×H),
134.   native resolution (W×H), and scaling factor of their primary
135.   display by hovering over the tray icon, without opening any
136.   menu.
137. - **SC-002**: Users can see the same resolution breakdown at the
138.   top of the right-click context menu every time they open it.
139. - **SC-003**: The displayed resolution values match the values shown
140.   in Windows Display Settings for the primary monitor in 100% of
141.   tested scaling configurations (100%, 125%, 150%, 175%, 200%,
142.   225%, 250%, 300%).
143. - **SC-004**: Resolution information updates within one poll cycle
144.   (default 5 seconds) after a display change event (dock, undock,
145.   scaling change).
146. - **SC-005**: The tooltip text fits within the system tooltip
147.   character limit and is fully readable without truncation.
148. 
149. ## Assumptions
150. 
151. - The tooltip format will follow a pattern like:
152.   `"Ultrawide | 1920×1080 effective (3840×2160 @ 200%)"` —
153.   exact wording may be refined during implementation as long as
154.   all four data points (effective W×H, native W×H, scaling, and
155.   active profile) are present.
156. - The context menu will present the resolution info as read-only
157.   (disabled/greyed) menu items at the top, consistent with the
158.   existing status item style.
159. - The existing poll/event mechanism for detecting display changes
160.   is sufficient; no new detection mechanism is needed.
161. - Only the primary display's resolution is shown (multi-monitor
162.   display is out of scope for this feature).
163. - Height values are derived by applying the same scaling formula
164.   used for width (physicalHeight × 96 / dpiY).
165.
```

</details>

<details>

```
1. # Data Model: Hover Effective Resolution
2. 
3. **Date**: 2026-03-06
4. **Feature**: 001-hover-effective-resolution
5. 
6. ## Entities
7. 
8. ### DisplayInfo
9. 
10. Represents the current state of the primary display as surfaced
11. to the user via tooltip and context menu. This is an ephemeral
12. data structure recomputed on each poll cycle — not persisted.
13. 
14. | Field | Type | Description |
15. |-------|------|-------------|
16. | EffectiveWidth | int | Logical width after DPI scaling (px) |
17. | EffectiveHeight | int | Logical height after DPI scaling (px) |
18. | NativeWidth | int | Physical display width (px) |
19. | NativeHeight | int | Physical display height (px) |
20. | ScalingPercent | int | Display scaling as whole % (e.g., 125) |
21. 
22. **Derivation rules**:
23. 
24. - `EffectiveWidth = NativeWidth × 96 / dpiX` (rounded to nearest int)
25. - `EffectiveHeight = NativeHeight × 96 / dpiY` (rounded to nearest int)
26. - `ScalingPercent = (int)Math.Round(dpiX / 96.0 × 100)`
27. 
28. **Identity**: Singleton — only one DisplayInfo exists at a time
29. (primary display only).
30. 
31. **Lifecycle**: Created fresh on each poll/event. No state
32. transitions — each instance is immutable and replaced entirely.
33. 
34. ## Relationships
35. 
36. - `DisplayInfo` is consumed by `TrayApplicationContext` to format
37.   the tooltip string and context menu status items.
38. - `DisplayInfo` is produced by `DisplayMonitor` which already
39.   owns the DPI detection and poll/event loop.
40. - The existing `EffectiveWidth` property on `DisplayMonitor` will
41.   be supplemented (not replaced) by the full `DisplayInfo`.
42.
```

</details>

```
1. # Research: Hover Effective Resolution
2. 
3. **Date**: 2026-03-06
4. **Feature**: 001-hover-effective-resolution
5. 
6. ## Summary
7. 
8. No NEEDS CLARIFICATION items existed in the Technical Context.
9. All technology choices are determined by the existing codebase.
10. Research was limited to confirming existing patterns.
11. 
12. ## Findings
13. 
14. ### Display Resolution Calculation
15. 
16. - **Decision**: Reuse existing `GetDpiForMonitor` P/Invoke to
17.   derive both effective width and effective height.
18. - **Rationale**: The call already returns `dpiX` and `dpiY`. The
19.   existing code discards `dpiY` (out parameter `_`). Capturing it
20.   enables height scaling with zero additional P/Invoke overhead.
21. - **Alternatives considered**: `GetDeviceCaps` via GDI — rejected
22.   because `GetDpiForMonitor` is already in `NativeMethods.cs` and
23.   is per-monitor aware.
24. 
25. ### Tooltip Character Budget
26. 
27. - **Decision**: Keep tooltip ≤128 chars using format like:
28.   `"Ultrawide | 1920×1080 eff (3840×2160 @ 200%)"` (~50 chars).
29. - **Rationale**: Windows `NotifyIcon.Text` has a 128-character
30.   limit. Even with long profile names, the format fits comfortably.
31. - **Alternatives considered**: Multi-line tooltip via
32.   `NOTIFYICONDATA` v6 balloon — rejected as over-engineered per
33.   Constitution Principle III.
34. 
35. ### Context Menu Layout
36. 
37. - **Decision**: Replace the existing single "Width: Xpx" disabled
38.   menu item with two or three disabled items showing effective
39.   resolution, native resolution, and scaling %, followed by the
40.   existing "Profile:" item and a separator.
41. - **Rationale**: Disabled menu items at the top is the existing
42.   pattern. No new UI paradigm needed.
43. - **Alternatives considered**: Submenu for resolution details —
44.   rejected as unnecessary nesting for three data points.
45. 
46. ### Height Calculation
47. 
48. - **Decision**: Use `Screen.PrimaryScreen.Bounds.Height` for
49.   physical height, then scale with `physicalHeight * 96.0 / dpiY`.
50. - **Rationale**: Mirrors the existing width calculation exactly.
51.   `Bounds.Height` returns physical pixels in a DPI-aware app.
52. - **Alternatives considered**: None — this is the standard approach.
53.
```

</details>

212. ## Implementation Strategy
213. 
214. ### MVP First (User Story 1 Only)
215. 
216. 1. Complete Phase 1: Setup
217. 2. Complete Phase 2: Foundational (CRITICAL - blocks all stories)
218. 3. Complete Phase 3: User Story 1
219. 4. **STOP and VALIDATE**: Test User Story 1 independently
220. 5. Deploy/demo if ready
221. 
222. ### Incremental Delivery
223. 
224. 1. Complete Setup + Foundational → Foundation ready
225. 2. Add User Story 1 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo (MVP!)
226. 3. Add User Story 2 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo
227. 4. Add User Story 3 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo
228. 5. Each story adds value without breaking previous stories
229. 
230. ### Parallel Team Strategy
231. 
232. With multiple developers:
233. 
234. 1. Team completes Setup + Foundational together
235. 2. Once Foundational is done:
236.    - Developer A: User Story 1
237.    - Developer B: User Story 2
238.    - Developer C: User Story 3
239. 3. Stories complete and integrate independently
240. 
241. ---
242. 
243. ## Notes
244. 
245. - [P] tasks = different files, no dependencies
246. - [Story] label maps task to specific user story for traceability
247. - Each user story should be independently completable and testable
248. - Verify tests fail before implementing
249. - Commit after each task or logical group
250. - Stop at any checkpoint to validate story independently
251. - Avoid: vague tasks, same file conflicts, cross-story dependencies that break independence
252.

1. using Microsoft.Win32;
2. 
3. namespace TaskbarAlignmentTool;
4. 
5. /// <summary>
6. /// Monitors the primary display's effective resolution using Windows messages
7. /// (WM_DISPLAYCHANGE, WM_DPICHANGED) and a low-frequency fallback timer.
8. /// </summary>
9. internal sealed class DisplayMonitor : IDisposable
10. {
11.     private readonly System.Windows.Forms.Timer _fallbackTimer;
12.     private readonly DisplayChangeWindow _messageWindow;
13.     private int _lastWidth;
14.     private ResolutionMode _resolutionMode;
15. 
16.     public int EffectiveWidth => GetWidth(_resolutionMode);
17. 
18.     public event EventHandler<int>? ResolutionChanged;
19. 
20.     public DisplayMonitor(int fallbackIntervalMs = 60000, ResolutionMode resolutionMode = ResolutionMode.Effective)
21.     {
22.         _resolutionMode = resolutionMode;
23.         _lastWidth = EffectiveWidth;
24. 
25.         // Hidden window to receive WM_DISPLAYCHANGE and WM_DPICHANGED
26.         _messageWindow = new DisplayChangeWindow(OnDisplayMessage);
27. 
28.         // Also subscribe to the .NET event for display settings changes
29.         SystemEvents.DisplaySettingsChanged += OnDisplaySettingsChanged;
30. 
31.         // Low-frequency fallback timer (default 60s) as a safety net
32.         _fallbackTimer = new System.Windows.Forms.Timer
33.         {
34.             Interval = Math.Max(fallbackIntervalMs, 5000)
35.         };
36.         _fallbackTimer.Tick += OnFallbackTick;
37.         _fallbackTimer.Start();
38.     }
39. 
40.     /// <summary>Updates the fallback timer interval and resolution mode at runtime.</summary>
41.     public void UpdateInterval(int intervalMs, ResolutionMode? resolutionMode = null)
42.     {
43.         _fallbackTimer.Interval = Math.Max(intervalMs, 5000);
44.         if (resolutionMode.HasValue)
45.             _resolutionMode = resolutionMode.Value;
46.     }
47. 
48.     private void OnDisplayMessage()
49.     {
50.         CheckAndNotify();
51.     }
52. 
53.     private void OnDisplaySettingsChanged(object? sender, EventArgs e)
54.     {
55.         CheckAndNotify();
56.     }
57. 
58.     private void OnFallbackTick(object? sender, EventArgs e)
59.     {
60.         CheckAndNotify();
61.     }
62. 
63.     private void CheckAndNotify()
64.     {
65.         var current = EffectiveWidth;
66.         if (current != _lastWidth)
67.         {
68.             _lastWidth = current;
69.             ResolutionChanged?.Invoke(this, current);
70.         }
71.     }
72. 
73.     /// <summary>Forces a re-check and fires the event if the width changed (or always if force is true).</summary>
74.     public void Refresh(bool force = false)
75.     {
76.         var current = EffectiveWidth;
77.         if (force || current != _lastWidth)
78.         {
79.             _lastWidth = current;
80.             ResolutionChanged?.Invoke(this, current);
81.         }
82.     }
83. 
84.     private static int GetWidth(ResolutionMode mode)
85.     {
86.         var screen = Screen.PrimaryScreen;
87.         if (screen == null) return 0;
88. 
89.         int physicalWidth = screen.Bounds.Width;
90. 
91.         if (mode == ResolutionMode.Physical)
92.             return physicalWidth;
93. 
94.         try
95.         {
96.             var hMonitor = NativeMethods.MonitorFromPoint(0, NativeMethods.MONITOR_DEFAULTTOPRIMARY);
97.             if (hMonitor != nint.Zero &&
98.                 NativeMethods.GetDpiForMonitor(hMonitor, NativeMethods.MDT_EFFECTIVE_DPI, out uint dpiX, out _) == 0 &&
99.                 dpiX > 0)
100.             {
101.                 return (int)(physicalWidth * 96.0 / dpiX);
102.             }
103.         }
104.         catch
105.         {
106.             // Fall through to physical width
107.         }
108. 
109.         return physicalWidth;
110.     }
111. 
112.     public void Dispose()
113.     {
114.         SystemEvents.DisplaySettingsChanged -= OnDisplaySettingsChanged;
115.         _fallbackTimer.Stop();
116.         _fallbackTimer.Dispose();
117.         _messageWindow.DestroyHandle();
118.     }
119. 
120.     /// <summary>
121.     /// Hidden NativeWindow that receives WM_DISPLAYCHANGE and WM_DPICHANGED messages.
122.     /// </summary>
123.     private sealed class DisplayChangeWindow : NativeWindow
124.     {
125.         private readonly Action _onChange;
126. 
127.         public DisplayChangeWindow(Action onChange)
128.         {
129.             _onChange = onChange;
130.             var cp = new CreateParams
131.             {
132.                 Caption = "TaskbarAlignmentTool_DisplayMonitor",
133.                 // Message-only window (HWND_MESSAGE parent)
134.                 Parent = new nint(-3)
135.             };
136.             CreateHandle(cp);
137.         }
138. 
139.         protected override void WndProc(ref Message m)
140.         {
141.             if (m.Msg is NativeMethods.WM_DISPLAYCHANGE or NativeMethods.WM_DPICHANGED)
142.             {
143.                 _onChange();
144.             }
145.             base.WndProc(ref m);
146.         }
147.     }
148. }
149.

private static int GetWidth(ResolutionMode mode)
{

        }
    }

### ✅ `view`

**C:\Users\duncanbeard\Personal\taskbar-alignment-tool\TaskbarAlignmentTool\DisplayMonitor.cs**

C:\Users\duncanbeard\Personal\taskbar-alignment-tool\TaskbarAlignmentTool\DisplayMonitor.cs

```
1. using Microsoft.Win32;
2. 
3. namespace TaskbarAlignmentTool;
4. 
5. /// <summary>
6. /// Snapshot of the primary display's resolution and scaling state.
7. /// Recomputed on each poll cycle — not persisted.
8. /// </summary>
9. internal sealed record DisplayInfo(
10.     int EffectiveWidth,
11.     int EffectiveHeight,
12.     int NativeWidth,
13.     int NativeHeight,
14.     int ScalingPercent);
15. 
16. /// <summary>
17. /// Monitors the primary display's effective resolution using Windows messages
18. /// (WM_DISPLAYCHANGE, WM_DPICHANGED) and a low-frequency fallback timer.
19. /// </summary>
20. internal sealed class DisplayMonitor : IDisposable
21. {
22.     private readonly System.Windows.Forms.Timer _fallbackTimer;
23.     private readonly DisplayChangeWindow _messageWindow;
24.     private int _lastWidth;
25.     private DisplayInfo _lastDisplayInfo;
26.     private ResolutionMode _resolutionMode;
27. 
28.     public int EffectiveWidth => GetWidth(_resolutionMode);
29. 
30.     public DisplayInfo CurrentDisplayInfo => GetDisplayInfo(_resolutionMode);
31. 
32.     public event EventHandler<int>? ResolutionChanged;
33.     public event EventHandler<DisplayInfo>? DisplayInfoChanged;
34. 
35.     public DisplayMonitor(int fallbackIntervalMs = 60000, ResolutionMode resolutionMode = ResolutionMode.Effective)
36.     {
37.         _resolutionMode = resolutionMode;
38.         _lastWidth = EffectiveWidth;
39.         _lastDisplayInfo = CurrentDisplayInfo;
40. 
41.         // Hidden window to receive WM_DISPLAYCHANGE and WM_DPICHANGED
42.         _messageWindow = new DisplayChangeWindow(OnDisplayMessage);
43. 
44.         // Also subscribe to the .NET event for display settings changes
45.         SystemEvents.DisplaySettingsChanged += OnDisplaySettingsChanged;
46. 
47.         // Low-frequency fallback timer (default 60s) as a safety net
48.         _fallbackTimer = new System.Windows.Forms.Timer
49.         {
50.             Interval = Math.Max(fallbackIntervalMs, 5000)
51.         };
52.         _fallbackTimer.Tick += OnFallbackTick;
53.         _fallbackTimer.Start();
54.     }
55. 
56.     /// <summary>Updates the fallback timer interval and resolution mode at runtime.</summary>
57.     public void UpdateInterval(int intervalMs, ResolutionMode? resolutionMode = null)
58.     {
59.         _fallbackTimer.Interval = Math.Max(intervalMs, 5000);
60.         if (resolutionMode.HasValue)
61.             _resolutionMode = resolutionMode.Value;
62.     }
63. 
64.     private void OnDisplayMessage()
65.     {
66.         CheckAndNotify();
67.     }
68. 
69.     private void OnDisplaySettingsChanged(object? sender, EventArgs e)
70.     {
71.         CheckAndNotify();
72.     }
73. 
74.     private void OnFallbackTick(object? sender, EventArgs e)
75.     {
76.         CheckAndNotify();
77.     }
78. 
79.     private void CheckAndNotify()
80.     {
81.         var current = EffectiveWidth;
82.         var currentInfo = CurrentDisplayInfo;
83.         if (current != _lastWidth || currentInfo != _lastDisplayInfo)
84.         {
85.             _lastWidth = current;
86.             _lastDisplayInfo = currentInfo;
87.             ResolutionChanged?.Invoke(this, current);
88.             DisplayInfoChanged?.Invoke(this, currentInfo);
89.         }
90.     }
91. 
92.     /// <summary>Forces a re-check and fires the event if the width changed (or always if force is true).</summary>
93.     public void Refresh(bool force = false)
94.     {
95.         var current = EffectiveWidth;
96.         var currentInfo = CurrentDisplayInfo;
97.         if (force || current != _lastWidth || currentInfo != _lastDisplayInfo)
98.         {
99.             _lastWidth = current;
100.             _lastDisplayInfo = currentInfo;
101.             ResolutionChanged?.Invoke(this, current);
102.             DisplayInfoChanged?.Invoke(this, currentInfo);
103.         }
104.     }
105. 
106.     private static int GetWidth(ResolutionMode mode)
107.     {
108.         var screen = Screen.PrimaryScreen;
109.         if (screen == null) return 0;
110. 
111.         int physicalWidth = screen.Bounds.Width;
112. 
113.         if (mode == ResolutionMode.Physical)
114.             return physicalWidth;
115. 
116.         try
117.         {
118.             var hMonitor = NativeMethods.MonitorFromPoint(0, NativeMethods.MONITOR_DEFAULTTOPRIMARY);
119.             if (hMonitor != nint.Zero &&
120.                 NativeMethods.GetDpiForMonitor(hMonitor, NativeMethods.MDT_EFFECTIVE_DPI, out uint dpiX, out _) == 0 &&
121.                 dpiX > 0)
122.             {
123.                 return (int)(physicalWidth * 96.0 / dpiX);
124.             }
125.         }
126.         catch
127.         {
128.             // Fall through to physical width
129.         }
130. 
131.         return physicalWidth;
132.     }
133. 
134.     private static DisplayInfo GetDisplayInfo(ResolutionMode mode)
135.     {
136.         var screen = Screen.PrimaryScreen;
137.         if (screen == null)
138.             return new DisplayInfo(0, 0, 0, 0, 100);
139. 
140.         int nativeWidth = screen.Bounds.Width;
141.         int nativeHeight = screen.Bounds.Height;
142. 
143.         if (mode == ResolutionMode.Physical)
144.             return new DisplayInfo(nativeWidth, nativeHeight, nativeWidth, nativeHeight, 100);
145. 
146.         try
147.         {
148.             var hMonitor = NativeMethods.MonitorFromPoint(0, NativeMethods.MONITOR_DEFAULTTOPRIMARY);
149.             if (hMonitor != nint.Zero &&
150.                 NativeMethods.GetDpiForMonitor(hMonitor, NativeMethods.MDT_EFFECTIVE_DPI, out uint dpiX, out uint dpiY) == 0 &&
151.                 dpiX > 0 && dpiY > 0)
152.             {
153.                 int effectiveWidth = (int)Math.Round(nativeWidth * 96.0 / dpiX);
154.                 int effectiveHeight = (int)Math.Round(nativeHeight * 96.0 / dpiY);
155.                 int scalingPercent = (int)Math.Round(dpiX / 96.0 * 100);
156.                 return new DisplayInfo(effectiveWidth, effectiveHeight, nativeWidth, nativeHeight, scalingPercent);
157.             }
158.         }
159.         catch
160.         {
161.             // Fall through to physical = effective
162.         }
163. 
164.         return new DisplayInfo(nativeWidth, nativeHeight, nativeWidth, nativeHeight, 100);
165.     }
166. 
167.     public void Dispose()
168.     {
169.         SystemEvents.DisplaySettingsChanged -= OnDisplaySettingsChanged;
170.         _fallbackTimer.Stop();
171.         _fallbackTimer.Dispose();
172.         _messageWindow.DestroyHandle();
173.     }
174. 
175.     /// <summary>
176.     /// Hidden NativeWindow that receives WM_DISPLAYCHANGE and WM_DPICHANGED messages.
177.     /// </summary>
178.     private sealed class DisplayChangeWindow : NativeWindow
179.     {
180.         private readonly Action _onChange;
181. 
182.         public DisplayChangeWindow(Action onChange)
183.         {
184.             _onChange = onChange;
185.             var cp = new CreateParams
186.             {
187.                 Caption = "TaskbarAlignmentTool_DisplayMonitor",
188.                 // Message-only window (HWND_MESSAGE parent)
189.                 Parent = new nint(-3)
190.             };
191.             CreateHandle(cp);
192.         }
193. 
194.         protected override void WndProc(ref Message m)
195.         {
196.             if (m.Msg is NativeMethods.WM_DISPLAYCHANGE or NativeMethods.WM_DPICHANGED)
197.             {
198.                 _onChange();
199.             }
200.             base.WndProc(ref m);
201.         }
202.     }
203. }
204. 
```
